Every\n// rank needs only its rows of A plus all of B; both are generated locally from\n// the deterministic initializer, so no input communication is required. The\n// result rows are gathered on rank 0.\n//\n// The local kernel is a packed, cache-blocked, register-tiled GEMM. For every\n// element C[i][j] the products A[i][k]*B[k][j] are accumulated in strictly\n// increasing k order starting from 0.0, exactly like the reference triple\n// loop, so the results are bitwise identical to the serial code.\n// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\ntypedef double v4d __attribute__((vector_size(32)));\n\nconstexpr size_t MR = 6;     // rows per micro tile\nconstexpr size_t NR = 8;     // cols per micro tile (2 x 4 doubles)\nconstexpr size_t KC = 256;   // k block\nconstexpr size_t MC = 96;    // row block (multiple of MR)\nconstexpr size_t NC = 2048;  // column block (multiple of NR)\n\nstatic inline size_t roundUp(size_t x, size_t m) { return (x + m - 1) / m * m; }\n\n// Pack all of B into column panels of width NR: Bp[panel][k][NR], zero padded.\nstatic void packB(const std::vector<double>& B, std::vector<double>& Bp, const size_t N) {\n    const size_t panels = roundUp(N, NR) / NR;\n    Bp.assign(panels * N * NR, 0.0);\n    for (size_t p = 0; p < panels; ++p) {\n        const size_t j0 = p * NR;\n        const size_t w = std::min(NR, N - j0);\n        double* dst = Bp.data() + p * N * NR;\n        for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) {\n            const double* src = B.data() + k * N + j0;\n            for (size_t c = 0; c < w; ++c) dst[k * NR + c] = src[c];\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// Pack local rows of A into row panels of height MR: Ap[panel][k][MR], zero padded.\nstatic void packA(const double* A, std::vector<double>& Ap, const size_t rows, const size_t N) {\n    const size_t panels = roundUp(rows, MR) / MR;\n    Ap.assign(panels * N * MR, 0.0);\n    for (size_t p = 0; p < panels; ++p) {\n        const size_t i0 = p * MR;\n        const size_t h = std::min(MR, rows - i0);\n        double* dst = Ap.data() + p * N * MR;\n        for (size_t r = 0; r < h; ++r) {\n            const double* src = A + (i0 + r) * N;\n            for (size_t k = 0; k < N; ++k) dst[k * MR + r] = src[k];\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n// acc[MR x NR] (in C) += Ap[kc x MR] * Bp[kc x NR], k in increasing order.\nstatic inline void microKernel(const size_t kc, const double* __restrict a, const double* __restrict b,\n                               double* __restrict c, const size_t ldc, const size_t h, const size_t w) {\n    v4d acc[MR][2];\n    if (h == MR && w == NR) {\n        for (size_t r = 0; r < MR; ++r) {\n            memcpy(&acc[r][0], c + r * ldc, sizeof(v4d));\n            memcpy(&acc[r][1], c + r * ldc + 4, sizeof(v4d));\n        }\n    } else {\n        double tmp[MR][NR] = {};\n        for (size_t r = 0; r < h; ++r)\n            for (size_t q = 0; q < w; ++q) tmp[r][q] = c[r * ldc + q];\n        for (size_t r = 0; r < MR; ++r) {\n            memcpy(&acc[r][0], &tmp[r][0], sizeof(v4d));\n            memcpy(&acc[r][1], &tmp[r][4], sizeof(v4d));\n        }\n    }\n\n    for (size_t k = 0; k < kc; ++k) {\n        v4d b0, b1;\n        memcpy(&b0, b + k * NR, sizeof(v4d));\n        memcpy(&b1, b + k * NR + 4, sizeof(v4d));\n        const double* ak = a + k * MR;\n#pragma GCC unroll 6\n        for (size_t r = 0; r < MR; ++r) {\n            const v4d av = {ak[r], ak[r], ak[r], ak[r]};\n            acc[r][0] += av * b0;\n            acc[r][1] += av * b1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    if (h == MR && w == NR) {\n        for (size_t r = 0; r < MR; ++r) {\n            memcpy(c + r * ldc, &acc[r][0], sizeof(v4d));\n            memcpy(c + r * ldc + 4, &acc[r][1], sizeof(v4d));\n        }\n    } else {\n        double tmp[MR][NR];\n        for (size_t r = 0; r < MR; ++r) {\n            memcpy(&tmp[r][0], &acc[r][0], sizeof(v4d));\n            memcpy(&tmp[r][4], &acc[r][1], sizeof(v4d));\n        }\n        for (size_t r = 0; r < h; ++r)\n            for (size_t q = 0; q < w; ++q) c[r * ldc + q] = tmp[r][q];\n    }\n}\n\n// Computes C[0:rows, :] = A[0:rows, :] * B for the local row block.\nvoid matrixMultiplyLocal(const double* A, const std::vector<double>& B, double* C,\n                         const size_t rows, const size_t N) {\n    if (rows == 0 || N == 0) return;\n    std::vector<double> Ap, Bp;\n    packA(A, Ap, rows, N);\n    packB(B, Bp, N);\n\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < rows * N; ++i) C[i] = 0.0;\n\n    for (size_t jc = 0; jc < N; jc += NC) {\n        const size_t jcEnd = std::min(N, jc + NC);\n        for (size_t pc = 0; pc < N; pc += KC) {\n            const size_t kc = std::min(KC, N - pc);\n            for (size_t ic = 0; ic < rows; ic += MC) {\n                const size_t icEnd = std::min(rows, ic + MC);\n                for (size_t jr = jc; jr < jcEnd; jr += NR) {\n                    const size_t w = std::min(NR, N - jr);\n                    const double* bp = Bp.data() + (jr / NR) * N * NR + pc * NR;\n                    for (size_t ir = ic; ir < icEnd; ir += MR) {\n                        const size_t h = std::min(MR, rows - ir);\n                        const double* ap = Ap.data() + (ir / MR) * N * MR + pc * MR;\n                        microKernel(kc, ap, bp, C + ir * N + jr, N, h, w);\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n'''\nsrc = src[:start] + kernel + src[end:]\nopen('matmul.cpp','w').write(src)\nEOF"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":574,"cache_read_input_tokens":18722,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":574},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"5775708f-f794-49e3-9907-e2f37badad9c","uuid":"6e77323a-d373-44a3-ae21-c27112ee627a","timestamp":"2026-10-05T08:40:15.989Z","request_id":"req_011CfinmL7SqWpXDtgUJNCi6"}

Only `matmul/matmul.cpp` and `matmul/CMakeLists.txt` were changed, and the executable is still called `matmul`.\n\n**Results match the original exactly.** I compared the hash from `-r` against the original binary for N = 1, 2, 3, 7, 13, 100, 257, 999, 1000, 1001 and 1537. I used several rank counts (1, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 12, 30, 64, 128), including more ranks than rows. Every case matched bit for bit, and `-v` passed every time.\n\n**Speed (this machine: 2 × 64-core EPYC 7763):**\n\n| N | Original (serial) | MPI, 1 rank | MPI, 128 ranks |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 2048 | 94,195 ms | 746 ms | 26 ms |\n| 8192 | — | — | 591 ms (~1.86 TFLOPS)* |\n\n\\* Spreading ranks across both sockets (`--map-by socket --bind-to core`) matters. With default placement, N=8192 on 128 ranks took about 1000 ms. Running 256 ranks (using hyper-threads) was slower than 128.\n\n**How it works:**\n- **Splitting the work:** the ranks are arranged in a near-square grid, and each one computes its own block of C. Each rank needs only its slice of A and its slice of B, so per-rank memory traffic shrinks as you add ranks. My first version split by rows only, which made every rank read all of B, and it stopped scaling past about 32 ranks.\n- **No input communication:** the inputs come from the deterministic generator, so each rank builds its own slices. Rank 0 still builds the full A and B so validation works as before.\n- **Collecting results:** rank 0 receives each block directly into place in C. That transfer is inside the timed region, so the reported time includes getting the full result to rank 0. Only rank 0 prints, and it passes the exit code to the other ranks.\n- **Local compute:** each rank runs a cache-blocked kernel using AVX2 vector instructions. It adds the terms for each element in the same order as the original loop.\n\n**Decision for you: rounding.** I first got exact matches only for N=512 and 2048 (powers of two). The cause is how GCC compiled the original loop: it rounds after each multiply and each add, except the last term when N is odd, which it does as a single fused multiply-add (FMA) on this CPU. To reproduce that, I added `-ffp-contract=off` to the build flags and wrote the odd-N fused last step explicitly in the kernel. This mimics this compiler's output, not anything in the C++ source. If you'd rather allow FMA everywhere, I can remove it: that is faster, but results would differ from the original in the last bits, while still passing `-v`.\n\nTo build and run: `cmake -S matmul -B build && cmake --build build && mpirun -np 128 --map-by socket --bind-to core build/matmul -n 8192 -v`."}],"container":null,"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":978,"cache_read_input_tokens":52565,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":978},"output_tokens":6,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"input_transformations":[],"diagnostics":null,"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"5775708f-f794-49e3-9907-e2f37badad9c","uuid":"c35a223a-1040-4a31-9b72-88f305e37872","timestamp":"2026-10-05T09:00:31.462Z","request_id":"req_011CfipKfEPVvFMfcdbATHjJ"}

The cause is how GCC compiled the original loop: it rounds after each multiply and each add, except the last term when N is odd, which it does as a single fused multiply-add (FMA) on this CPU. To reproduce that, I added `-ffp-contract=off` to the build flags and wrote the odd-N fused last step explicitly in the kernel. This mimics this compiler's output, not anything in the C++ source. If you'd rather allow FMA everywhere, I can remove it: that is faster, but results would differ from the original in the last bits, while still passing `-v`.\n\nTo build and run: `cmake -S matmul -B build && cmake --build build && mpirun -np 128 --map-by socket --bind-to core build/matmul -n 8192 -v`.","ttft_ms":1959,"type":"result","duration_ms":1379265,"uuid":"c12af377-63a0-46c4-9e1e-2c94bc9ed210","ttft_stream_ms":1323,"time_to_request_ms":138,"first_content_frame_ms":1323,"queued_turn_count":0,"result_index":0}
